Mathlib.LinearAlgebra.AffineSpace.AffineEquiv

AffineEquivstructureMathlib.LinearAlgebra.AffineSpace.AffineEquiv, type
Wording
affine equivalence of x and x1 (from the name)
English
Let $k$, $x$ and $x_{1}$ be sets. Then the affine equivalences of $x$ and $x_{1}$ are a basic type.
Formula
$k , x , x_{1} \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{AffineEquiv} (k , x , x_{1})\ \mathsf{set}$

Then the left direct sum of $x_{5}$ is a linear equivalence of the set of tensor products of the set of direct sums of $x_{3}$ and the function that maps $x_{12}$ to $x_{5}$ applied to $x_{12}$ and $x_{6}$ and the set of direct sums of $x_{3}$ and the function that maps $i$ to the set of tensor products of $x_{5}$ applied to $i$ and $x_{6}$ defined as the composition of the composition of the congruence of $1$ and the inverse of $\operatorname{lid}$ applied to $R$, $x_{6}$ and $\mathrm{PUnit}$ and the direct sum of $x_{5}$ and the function that maps $x_{12}$ to $x_{6}$ and the left linear equivalence congruence of $S$ and $\operatorname{prodUnique}$ applied to $x_{3}$ and $\mathrm{Unit}$.
Formula
$R , S \in \mathrm{Set} , x_{5} \in x_{3} \rightarrow \mathrm{Set} , x_{6} \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{directSumLeft} (R , S , x_{5} , x_{6}):= \operatorname{trans} (\operatorname{trans} (\operatorname{congr} (1 , \operatorname{symm} (\operatorname{lid} (R , x_{6} , \mathrm{PUnit}))), \operatorname{directSum} (R , S , x_{5} , \lambda x_{12} . x_{6})), \operatorname{lequivCongrLeft} (S , \operatorname{prodUnique} (x_{3} , \mathrm{Unit})))\in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (\operatorname{id} (S), \mathrm{TensorProduct} (R , \mathrm{DirectSum} (x_{3} , \lambda x_{12} . x_{5} (x_{12})), x_{6}), \mathrm{DirectSum} (x_{3} , \lambda i . \mathrm{TensorProduct} (R , x_{5} (i), x_{6})))$
TensorProduct.directSumRightdefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.DirectSum.TensorProduct, function or constant
Wording
the right direct sum of x6 (from the name)
English
Let $R$, $S$ and $x_{5}$ be sets. Let $x_{6}$ be a function from elements of $x_{3}$ to sets. Then the right direct sum of $x_{6}$ is a linear equivalence of the set of tensor products of $x_{5}$ and the set of direct sums of $x_{3}$ and the function that maps $i$ to $x_{6}$ applied to $i$ and the set of direct sums of $x_{3}$ and the function that maps $i_{1}$ to the set of tensor products of $x_{5}$ and $x_{6}$ applied to $i_{1}$ defined as the composition of the composition of the congruence of the inverse of $\operatorname{lid}$ applied to $S$, $x_{5}$ and $\mathrm{PUnit}$ and $1$ and the direct sum of the function that maps $x_{12}$ to $x_{5}$ and $x_{6}$ and the left linear equivalence congruence of $S$ and the unique product of $x_{3}$ and $\mathrm{Unit}$.
Formula
$R , S , x_{5} \in \mathrm{Set} , x_{6} \in x_{3} \rightarrow \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{directSumRight} (R , S , x_{5} , x_{6}):= \operatorname{trans} (\operatorname{trans} (\operatorname{congr} (\operatorname{symm} (\operatorname{lid} (S , x_{5} , \mathrm{PUnit})), 1), \operatorname{directSum} (R , S , \lambda x_{12} . x_{5} , x_{6})), \operatorname{lequivCongrLeft} (S , \operatorname{uniqueProd} (x_{3} , \mathrm{Unit})))\in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (\operatorname{id} (S), \mathrm{TensorProduct} (R , x_{5} , \mathrm{DirectSum} (x_{3} , \lambda i . x_{6} (i))), \mathrm{DirectSum} (x_{3} , \lambda i_{1} . \mathrm{TensorProduct} (R , x_{5} , x_{6} (i_{1}))))$

Mathlib.LinearAlgebra.Dual.BaseChange

IsBaseChange.toDualdefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.Dual.BaseChange, function or constant
Wording
the base change of ibc (from the docstring)
English
Assume that $j$ is a base change. Then the base change of $\mathrm{ibc}$ is a linear map of the set of duals of $R$ and $V$ and the set of duals of $A$ and $W$ defined as the linear map left right homomorphism of $\mathrm{ibc}$ and the linear map of $R$ and $A$.
Formula
$\mathrm{IsBaseChange} (A , j)\vdash \operatorname{toDual} (\mathrm{ibc}):= \operatorname{linearMapLeftRightHom} (\mathrm{ibc} , \operatorname{linearMap} (R , A))\in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), \operatorname{Dual} (R , V), \operatorname{Dual} (A , W))$
IsBaseChange.toDualBaseChangedefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.Dual.BaseChange, function or constant
Wording
the dual base change of ibc (from the name)
English
Assume that $j$ is a base change. Then the dual base change of $\mathrm{ibc}$ is a linear equivalence of the set of tensor products of $A$ and the set of duals of $R$ and $V$ and the set of duals of $A$ and $W$.
Formula
$\mathrm{IsBaseChange} (A , j)\vdash \operatorname{toDualBaseChange} (\mathrm{ibc})\in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (\operatorname{id} (A), \mathrm{TensorProduct} (R , A , \operatorname{Dual} (R , V)), \operatorname{Dual} (A , W))$
Module.Dual.baseChangedefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.Dual.BaseChange, function or constant
Wording
the base change of A (from the name)
English
Let $A$ be a set. Then the base change of $A$ is a linear map of the set of duals of $R$ and $V$ and the set of duals of $A$ and the set of tensor products of $A$ and $V$ defined as the composition of the right composition of $R$ and $\operatorname{toLinearMap}$ applied to $\operatorname{rid}$ applied to $R$, $A$ and $A$ and the base change homomorphism of $V$ and $R$.
Formula
$A \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{baseChange} (A):= \operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{compRight} (R , \operatorname{toLinearMap} (\operatorname{rid} (R , A , A))), \operatorname{baseChangeHom} (R , A , V , R))\in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), \operatorname{Dual} (R , V), \operatorname{Dual} (A , \mathrm{TensorProduct} (R , A , V)))$
Module.Dual.congrdefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.Dual.BaseChange, function or constant
Wording
the congruence of e (from the name)
English
Let $e$ be a linear equivalence of $V$ and $W$. Then the congruence of $e$ is a linear equivalence of the set of duals of $R$ and $V$ and the set of duals of $R$ and $W$ defined as the left congruence of $e$.
Formula
$e \in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (\operatorname{id} (R), V , W)\vdash \operatorname{congr} (e):= \operatorname{congrLeft} (R , R , e)\in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (\operatorname{id} (R), \operatorname{Dual} (R , V), \operatorname{Dual} (R , W))$
